##  7.1 Live preview [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:live-preview "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:live-preview")
Unless it has become your muscle memory to click on the `Knit` button in RStudio, you may try to switch to `litedown::roam()` to preview your HTML output by clicking on the `run` buttons in the user interface. Note that the preview takes place _in memory only_. Although you see an HTML page rendered from a file in the preview, the page is not rendered to disk, unless you click on the `render` button ([7.1.3](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-buttons "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-buttons")), or call `litedown::fuse()` ([2](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:fuse "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:fuse")) or `mark()` ([4](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:mark "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:mark")) on the file.
###  7.1.1 Live reload [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:live-reload "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:live-reload")
By default, the preview will automatically refresh the content after you edit and save a file. If you prefer building the document only when you want to, you can turn off the live preview via `litedown::roam(live = FALSE)`. In this case, the document will be rebuilt only when you refresh the page by yourself.
###  7.1.2 The file listing [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-file-listing "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-file-listing")
After launching the preview via `litedown::roam()`, a file listing will be displayed, which shows the first few lines of `*.R`, `*.Rmd`, and `*.md` files in boxes, followed by the list of other files.
By clicking on plain-text files, you will see their full content. For binary files, they may be opened in your browser; if they cannot be opened, the browser may prompt to download the file.
Each file will have its size displayed after the filename, with a link attached to the file size. The link points to the raw file (the behavior of the link is up to the browser—the file may be opened or downloaded), and **litedown** will not process it at all.
The full path of the file or directory being previewed is displayed at the top left.
For `.R`, `.Rmd`, and `.md` files, you can click on the “Run” button (⏵) to render them to HTML in memory and preview the output. This button is displayed at the top right of each file box on the listing page, and at the top right of the preview page of an individual file.
Rendering `.R` and `.Rmd` files via the `Run` button means _the full R code_ in them will be executed. If the R code involves intensive computing, it may not be a good idea to run the whole file, unless you have taken measures to speed up the computing (e.g., via caching).
###  7.1.3 The buttons [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-buttons "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-buttons")
There is a button group at the top right of the preview page.
  * The `render` button (with the lightning or voltage icon, ↯): Render a document or project in a new R session and save the output file(s) to disk, which is similar to what the `Knit` button does in RStudio.
You can also use the keyboard shortcut `Ctrl` + `K` (or `Command` + `K` on macOS).
  * The `edit` buttons (with a pencil icon, ✎): Open the source file of the current page in your default editor if possible.
  * The `back`/`forward` buttons (with arrow icons, ← and →): Go back/forward through the browsing history, similar to the back/forward buttons on web browser toolbars.
You can also use the keyboard shortcuts `Alt` + `←` / `Alt` + `→`.
  * The `add` button (with the plus icon, +): Create a new `.Rmd` / `.md` / `.R` file with selected HTML features ([7.1.4](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:new-file "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:new-file")).
  * The `refresh` button (with the refresh icon, ⟳): Refresh the page. If you are previewing an `.Rmd` file, refreshing the page will rebuild it.
You can also use the keyboard shortcut `Ctrl` + `R` (or `Command` + `R` on macOS).
  * The `printer` button (with a printer icon, ⎙): Bring up the printer dialog, e.g., to print the page to PDF.


Code blocks in the preview mode will have line numbers automatically added to their left. If you click on a line number, it will bring you to that line in the source document.
Note that the keyboard shortcuts require the page to be currently on focus before they can take effect. This is important when you are viewing a page inside RStudio or other IDEs, because the viewer may not gain focus automatically, and you will have to explicitly click on it.
###  7.1.4 Creating new files [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:new-file "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:new-file")
The `add` button (+) opens a dialog to create a new file. You type a filename in the input box (a dropdown list of existing files is provided, and existing filenames are marked with ❌ so that you do not accidentally overwrite them). If you do not provide a filename extension, `.Rmd` will be appended automatically.
For `.Rmd` / `.md` / `.R` files, you can also select HTML features via checkboxes, such as callouts ([5.6](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:callout "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:callout")), tabsets ([5.4](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:tabsets "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:tabsets")), and the article format ([5.8](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:article "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:article")). See [4](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:assets "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:assets") for the full list of features. The corresponding CSS/JS assets will be added to the YAML metadata of the new file automatically.
The new file will be seeded with a YAML frontmatter containing a placeholder title, the author name (detected from the system username), and the current date, so you can start writing immediately.
###  7.1.5 Rendering a whole project [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:rendering-a-whole-project "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:rendering-a-whole-project")
When you preview the index file of a book or website project ([8](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:sites "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:sites")), the `render` button (↯) will render the whole project (via `fuse_book()` or `fuse_site()`) in a new R session and write the output to disk.
###  7.1.6 Cleaning up [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:cleaning-up "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:cleaning-up")
Previewing `.Rmd` and `.R` files that generate plots will leave `*__files/` directories (containing plot files) on disk by default. If you want to clean up such directories when closing or navigating away from the preview page, you may set the option

```
options(litedown.roam.cleanup = TRUE)

```

before you run `litedown::roam()` or in your `.Rprofile`. Note that it will not clean up a `*__files/` directory if it has existed before you preview a file. This is to make sure `roam()` will not delete existing files by accident. If you are certain that a `*__files/` directory can be safely deleted, you can always delete it by hand. After that, `roam()` will automatically clean it up when you preview the file again.
Please also note that when caching is enabled (via the chunk option `cache = TRUE`) in the file being previewed, the `*__files/` directory will not be cleaned up, because when a code chunk is cached, it will not be re-evaluated or re-generate plots next time (unless the cache is invalidated).
##  7.2 Visual editor [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:visual-editor "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:visual-editor")
Since the Markdown syntax of **litedown** can be viewed as a subset of Pandoc’s Markdown, you can use RStudio’s visual Markdown editor to author documents. Please bear in mind that most common, but not all, Markdown features are supported.
##  7.3 The `Knit` button [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:knit-button "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:knit-button")
If you use the RStudio IDE, the `Knit` button can render R Markdown to a **litedown** output format specified in YAML (e.g., `html` or `latex`). Please also remember to add a top-level setting `knit: litedown:::knit` in YAML, otherwise RStudio will either throw an error if you use the output format `html` or `latex`, or use `rmarkdown::render()` instead of `litedown::fuse()` to render the document.

```
---
output: html
knit: litedown:::knit
---

```

[Edit this chapter](https://github.com/yihui/litedown/edit/main//home/runner/work/litedown/litedown/docs/08-site.Rmd "https://github.com/yihui/litedown/edit/main//home/runner/work/litedown/litedown/docs/08-site.Rmd") on GitHub
#  8 Books and Websites [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:sites "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:sites")
> I can at least listen without indignation to the critic who is of the opinion that when one surveys the aims of cultural endeavour and the means it employs, one is bound to come to the conclusion that the whole effort is not worth the trouble, and that the outcome of it can only be a state of affairs which the individual will be unable to tolerate.
> —Sigmund Freud, _Civilization and Its Discontents_
  * [8.1 Creating a new project](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:creating-a-new-project "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:creating-a-new-project")
  * [8.2 Books](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:books "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:books")
    * [8.2.1 Previewing a single chapter](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:previewing-a-single-chapter "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:previewing-a-single-chapter")
  * [8.3 Websites](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:websites "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:websites")
    * [8.3.1 Default assets and layout](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:site-defaults "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:site-defaults")
  * [8.4 R package documentation](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site")
    * [8.4.1 The `pkg_*()` helper functions](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-pkg-helper-functions "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-pkg-helper-functions")
    * [8.4.2 Package sites via `pkg_site()`](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun")
    * [8.4.3 Package sites via GitHub Action](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha")
      * [8.4.3.1 Package playground](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:package-playground "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:package-playground")


Books and websites are usually based on multiple input files under a directory. For a directory to be recognized as a book or website project, it needs to contain a configuration file named `_litedown.yml`.
The project type (book or website) is determined by the presence of a top-level `book` or `site` field in `_litedown.yml`. If both fields exist, or you want to be explicit, you can set the top-level `type` field to either `book` or `site`, e.g.,

```
type: book
book:
  new_session: true

```

If you want to customize the output formats for books or websites, you should do it in `_litedown.yml`, e.g.,

```
output:
  html:
    options:
      toc:
        depth: 4
  latex:
    meta:
      documentclass: "book"

```

##  8.1 Creating a new project [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:creating-a-new-project "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:creating-a-new-project")
If you use RStudio, you can create a new litedown book or website project from the menu `File` > `New Project` > `New Directory` > `Project using litedown`. You will be able to select the project type (book or site) from the dropdown menu.
Alternatively, you can create a minimal project with the internal helper `litedown:::proj_skeleton()`:

```
litedown:::proj_skeleton("my-book", type = "book")
litedown:::proj_skeleton("my-site", type = "site")

```

The book skeleton contains an `index.Rmd` (the first chapter with YAML metadata), a sample chapter `01-intro.Rmd`, and a `_litedown.yml` config file. The site skeleton contains `index.Rmd`, `about.Rmd`, and `_litedown.yml`. You can build the book with `fuse_book()` and the site with `fuse_site()`. The configuration options are documented in later sections of this chapter.
##  8.2 Books [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:books "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:books")
The `_litedown.yml` file should contain a top-level field named `book`, which currently supports these options:

```
book:
  input: null
  new_session: false
  subdir: false
  pattern: "[.]R?md$"
  chapter_before: "Information before a chapter."
  chapter_after: "This chapter was generated from `$input$`."
  time: null

```

You can choose whether to render each input file in a new R session, whether to search subdirectories for input files, the types of input files (e.g., you can use `.md` or `.R` files if you want), and additional information to be included before/after each chapter, in which you can use some variables such as `$input$`, which is the path of each input file.
The `time` option provides a convenient way to collect timing data ([2.4.4](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:timing-data "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:timing-data")) across all chapters of a book. It takes a file path where the timing data is stored (a relative path is interpreted as relative to the first input file’s cache directory). This is equivalent to setting the `time` chunk option to the same file path in every chapter, but easier to configure in one place. With this option set, you can print `litedown::timing_data()` in the last chapter to see the timing data for the whole book. Remember to delete the data file when you no longer need it.
###  8.2.1 Previewing a single chapter [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:previewing-a-single-chapter "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:previewing-a-single-chapter")
Rendering a whole book may be time-consuming12
12 Remember that you can cache time-consuming code chunks to make the rendering faster. 
and unnecessary when you work on a book. It may be easier to only preview the single chapter that you currently work on. The preview can be done with `litedown::roam()`: 
  * If you run the index file (e.g., `index.Rmd`), the whole book will be previewed.
  * If you run other chapter files, only the specific chapter is previewed.


When a chapter contains dependencies on certain elements in other chapters (e.g., a chapter includes cross-references to other chapters), we recommend that you run the index file to preview the whole book at least once before you preview individual chapters, to make **litedown** know the book elements fully.
Similarly, if you use the configuration `new_session: false` to render all chapters in the same R session, and a later chapter uses computed results from a previous chapter, you will need to preview the full book at least once to make sure the results are computed and exist in the R session.
You may also choose to run the index file to preview the whole book but then work on an individual chapter. In this case, `roam()` will try to detect changes in chapter files. When a certain chapter file has been updated, its output on the full book page will be updated. That is, the full book page is _partially_ updated without being reloaded. This method lets you preview changes in one chapter while presenting the whole book. However, please note that certain JS libraries may not work well in this preview mode. When in doubt, refresh the page.
##  8.3 Websites [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:websites "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:websites")
The `_litedown.yml` file should contain a top-level field named `site`, and you are likely to customize the `meta` variables `css`, `include_before`, and `include_after` for the `html` format, e.g.,

```
site:
  rebuild: "outdated"
  pattern: "[.]R?md$"
  standalone: ["slides-*.Rmd"]

output:
  html:
    meta:
      css: ["@default"]
      include_before: "[Home](/) [About](/about.html)"
      include_after: "&copy; 2024 | [Edit]($input$)"

```

Basically, `include_before` can take a file or text input that will be used as the header of each web page, and `include_after` will be the footer.
The `rebuild` option controls when a page should be rebuilt:
  * `"outdated"` (default): Rebuild a page if the output file is older than the input file (i.e., the input has been modified since the last build).
  * `"newfile"`: Rebuild a page only if the output file does not exist yet (i.e., never rebuild existing output).
  * A number: Rebuild a page if the output file is older than the input file by more than this number of seconds. This is a more relaxed version of `"outdated"` (which is equivalent to `0`), useful to avoid rebuilding pages for tiny modification-time differences.


The `standalone` option takes a character vector of filename patterns (supporting wildcards like `*` and `?`). Files matching any pattern will be built with a plain `fuse()` call without site-level CSS, JS, navigation, or footer being injected. This is useful for standalone pages such as slides that need their own self-contained styles and should not inherit the site layout.
###  8.3.1 Default assets and layout [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:site-defaults "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:site-defaults")
When building a website, **litedown** applies a set of default CSS/JS assets and layout elements so that pages look reasonable without any configuration:
  * The default CSS assets are `@default`, `@article`, `@copy-button`, `@heading-anchor`, and `@pages`, and the default JS assets are `@sidenotes`, `@appendix`, `@toc-highlight`, `@copy-button`, `@heading-anchor`, and `@pages`. In other words, each page uses the article format ([5.8](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:article "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:article")) by default.
  * If `include_before` is not provided, a navigation menu is automatically generated from the top-level input files under the site root (plus any one-level subdirectories that contain an `index.html`, such as `playground/index.html`). The menu titles are derived from the filenames (e.g., `about.Rmd` becomes “About”, and `index.Rmd` becomes “Home”).
  * If `include_after` is not provided, a copyright footer (`© <year>`) is automatically added.
  * The options `embed_resources` ([4.1.5](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:embed-resources "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:embed-resources")) and `toc` ([4.1.15](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:toc "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:toc")) default to `false` and `true`, respectively.


If you want to customize the assets, you should provide the `css` and `js` meta variables as usual, which will _override_ the defaults. If you want to _add_ to the default assets instead of replacing them, use the `css2` and `js2` variables (which are appended to the built-in defaults), e.g.,

```
output:
  html:
    meta:
      css2: ["custom.css"]
      js2: ["custom.js"]

```

Absolute paths in links (i.e., paths starting with `/`) are automatically rewritten to relative paths, so a link like `[Home](/index.html)` will work correctly no matter which subdirectory the current page is in.
##  8.4 R package documentation [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site")
R package developers can build the full package documentation as either a book or a website.
###  8.4.1 The `pkg_*()` helper functions [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-pkg-helper-functions "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:the-pkg-helper-functions")
A series of helper functions have been provided in **litedown** to get various information about the package, such as the package description (`pkg_desc()`), news (`pkg_news()`), citation (`pkg_citation()`), source code (`pkg_code()`), and all manual pages (`pkg_manual()`). You can call these functions in code chunks to print out the desired information.
For example, you may call them in the appendix ([3.3.6](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:appendices "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:appendices")) of a book:

```
# Appendix {.appendix}

# Package Metadata

```{r, echo = FALSE}
litedown::pkg_desc()
```

To cite the package:

```{r, echo = FALSE}
litedown::pkg_citation()
```

# News

```{r, echo = FALSE}
litedown::pkg_news(recent = 0)  # show full news
```

# Manual pages

```{r, echo = FALSE}
litedown::pkg_manual()
```

# Source code

```{r, echo = FALSE}
litedown::pkg_code()
```

```

Alternatively, you can build a package website by calling these functions in separate `.Rmd` files, e.g., `pkg_desc()` in `index.Rmd`, `pkg_news()` in `news.Rmd`, and so on. You do not have to write these files yourself, since **litedown** ships a site template that does it for you ([8.4.2](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun")).
###  8.4.2 Package sites via `pkg_site()` [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun")
`pkg_site()` builds a website for an R package from a template bundled with **litedown**. Run it in the package root directory:

```
litedown::pkg_site()

```

Like the other `pkg_*()` functions, it detects the package from the `DESCRIPTION` file in the current or an upper-level directory, which can also be specified via the `name` argument. It copies the template to the `site/` directory (which can be changed via the `output` argument), deletes the pages that do not apply to the package, and renders the site with `fuse_site()`. The package has to be installed first, because rendering the site requires the installed version of it, e.g., `pkg_manual()` reads its help database, and `articles.Rmd` lists its built vignettes.
The template lives under `system.file('site', package = 'litedown')` and contains the following `.Rmd` files (explained in [5](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:pkg-site "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:pkg-site")):

```
list.files(system.file('site', package = 'litedown'), '[.]Rmd$')

```

```
#> [1] "_footer.Rmd"  "articles.Rmd" "code.Rmd"     "examples.Rmd" "index.Rmd"   
#> [6] "manual.Rmd"   "news.Rmd"    

```

5 Explanations of site source files.  
| source  | description  | content  |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
| `index`  | home page  |  `pkg_desc()`, `pkg_citation()`, and `README.md`  |  
| `articles`  | package vignettes  | the `doc/` folder of the installed package  |  
| `code`  | source code (R, C, C++, etc.)  | `pkg_code()`  |  
| `examples`  | examples  | the `examples/` folder in the package root directory  |  
| `manual`  | help pages  | `pkg_manual()`  |  
| `news`  | news  | `pkg_news()`  |  
| `_footer`  | page footer  | package authors  |  
Files that already exist in the site directory are never overwritten by the template, so you can customize any page by putting your own version of it in the site directory and committing it to your repository.
The `exclude` argument allows you to exclude certain pages from the site. By default, `code.Rmd` is excluded, and
  * `articles.Rmd` is excluded if the package does not have the `vignettes/` directory;
  * `examples.Rmd` is excluded if the package does not have the `examples/` directory;
  * `news.Rmd` is excluded if neither `NEWS.md` nor `inst/NEWS.Rd` is found in the package.


For example, if you do not want to include the news (or the source code, either), you can use `exclude = c('code.Rmd', 'news.Rmd')`.
If the package contains a book project (i.e., `docs/_litedown.yml` exists), the book will be built with `fuse_book()` and published under the `book/` subpage of the site.
If you publish the site to GitHub Pages yourself instead of using the GitHub action ([8.4.3](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha")), remember to create an empty `.nojekyll` file in the site directory, because Jekyll ignores files and directories whose names start with `_`, such as the `_footer.md` used by the template.
###  8.4.3 Package sites via GitHub Action [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-gha")
You may use GitHub Actions to automatically build and deploy package websites. The key is to call `litedown::pkg_site()`. I have provided [a simple GitHub action](https://github.com/yihui/actions/blob/main/litedown-site/action.yml "https://github.com/yihui/actions/blob/main/litedown-site/action.yml") in the **actions** repository, and you can add a step `uses: yihui/actions/litedown-site@HEAD` to your GitHub workflow to use it.
Remember to enable the deployment of GitHub Pages via GitHub Actions in your repository settings (`Settings` → `Pages` → `Build and deployment` → `Source` → `GitHub Actions`).
Below is [the full workflow](https://github.com/yihui/litedown/blob/main/.github/workflows/github-pages.yml "https://github.com/yihui/litedown/blob/main/.github/workflows/github-pages.yml") for building and publishing **litedown** ’s site to GitHub Pages:

```
name: Build and deploy package site

on:
  push:
    branches: ["main"]

permissions:
  contents: write

jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@HEAD
      - uses: r-lib/actions/setup-r@HEAD
        with:
          use-public-rspm: true
      - uses: yihui/actions/setup-r-dependencies@HEAD
        with:
          extra-packages: litedown xfun .
          r-universe: yihui
      - uses: yihui/actions/litedown-site@HEAD
        with:
          site-dir: 'site'
      - name: Deploy to gh-pages
        run: |
          tmp=$(mktemp -d)
          cp -r site/. "$tmp"
          git config user.name "github-actions[bot]"
          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
          if git fetch origin gh-pages; then
            git switch gh-pages
          else
            git switch --orphan gh-pages
            git rm -rf . 2>/dev/null || true
          fi
          find . -maxdepth 1 ! -name .git ! -name . -exec rm -rf {} +
          cp -r "$tmp"/. .
          git add -A
          git diff --cached --quiet || (git commit -m "deploy site" && git push origin gh-pages)

```

You can copy it to the `.github/workflows/` directory of the R package’s Git repository and commit/push it via Git. Within a few minutes, the package site should be ready at `https://user.github.io/pkg/`, where `user` is your GitHub username, and `pkg` is the repository name, e.g., [yihui.github.io/litedown/](https://yihui.github.io/litedown/ "https://yihui.github.io/litedown/").
The action `yihui/actions/litedown-site` supports a few options that you can configure under the `with` field. In the above example workflow, we used the option `site-dir`. Below are the options currently supported:
  * `site-dir`: Directory of the site source (default: `site`).
  * `pkg-root`: Root directory of the R package in the repository (default: `.`).
  * `exclude`: Pages to be excluded (filenames separated by spaces) (default: `code.Rmd`).
  * `cleanup`: Command to clean up the site directory before publishing, e.g., you can delete the `.Rmd` source files (default: `rm -f *.Rmd *.yml _*`).
  * `repo`: Whether to also publish the package as a minimal R package repository under the site (builds the source tarball into `src/contrib` and generates the `PACKAGES` index, so users can install via `install.packages()` with the site URL as `repos`) (default: `false`).
  * `repo-fields`: Additional DESCRIPTION fields to include in the `PACKAGES` index when `repo: true` (space- or comma-separated), passed to the `fields` argument of `tools::write_PACKAGES()`. For example, use `Revision` to surface a recorded git commit (default: ``).


The options `site-dir` and `exclude` are passed to the `output` and `exclude` arguments of `pkg_site()` ([8.4.2](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:pkg-site-fun")), respectively, except that `exclude` takes filenames separated by spaces here, e.g., `exclude: 'code.Rmd news.Rmd'`. For `pkg-root`, the action changes to this directory (or the subdirectory containing `DESCRIPTION`) before calling `pkg_site()`. The `cleanup` option allows you to run a command before publishing the site, e.g., you can delete the `.Rmd` source and `.yml` config files.
By default, the site is built under the `site/` directory of the package. If you change this path, please remember to adjust the `path` option for the `actions/upload-pages-artifact` action accordingly.
####  8.4.3.1 Package playground [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:package-playground "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:package-playground")
The site template also includes a `playground/` subpage, which provides an interactive R playground powered by [webR](https://r-wasm.org "https://r-wasm.org/"). It installs **litedown** and its dependencies in the browser and lets users write and run R code or render R Markdown documents without a server or a local R installation (yes, it works on your mobile device, too!).
The playground can be customized by adding a `playground/` directory to the package root, which `pkg_site()` copies into `site/playground/`. The following files are recognized:
  * `_default.Rmd` (or `_default.R`): the content pre-loaded in the editor when the playground opens. The `.Rmd` version is tried first; if it does not exist, the `.R` version is tried.
  * `setup.R`: an R script executed once in the webR session after packages are installed. It is a good place to install/load the package under documentation13
13 To install the development version of a package, [r-universe.dev](https://docs.r-universe.dev/install/binaries.html "https://docs.r-universe.dev/install/binaries.html") can be helpful. 
and define any helpers that users might need. 


If the package has an `examples/` directory, the `.Rmd`/`.R` files under it will show up in the “Load example…” dropdown of the playground. Selecting an example loads its source into the editor.

# Appendix [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:appendix "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#chp:appendix")

##  A.1 For rmarkdown users [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:rmarkdown "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:rmarkdown")
The **litedown** package can partially recognize the output format names `html_document`, `html_vignette`, and `pdf_document` from **rmarkdown** : Some options of these output formats are mapped to **litedown** ’s options internally.
For example, for an R Markdown document with the following output format:

```
output:
  html_document:
    toc: true
    number_sections: true
    anchor_sections: true
    self_contained: false

```

It will be transformed to:

```
output:
  html:
    options:
      toc: true
      number_sections: true
      embed_resources: false
    meta:
      css: ["default", "@heading-anchor"]
      js: ["@heading-anchor"]

```

Note that not all **rmarkdown** options are supported, and not even all supported options have exactly the same effects in **litedown**. The supported options include: `toc`, `toc_depth`, `number_sections`, `anchor_sections`, `code_folding`, `self_contained`, `math_method`, `css`, and `includes`.
##  A.2 File or text output [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:file-text "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:file-text")
By default, `mark()` and `fuse()` follow the rule of “file in, file out; text in, text out”. That is, if you pass an input file, you get an output file. If you pass text input, you get text output. For example:

```
fuse('foo.Rmd')  # returns a file 'foo.html' or 'foo.tex', etc.
mark('Hello _world_')  # returns text
mark(text = 'Hello _world_')  # text

```

You always get a file output if the argument `output` is a file path. If the `output` argument is not a character string (e.g., `NA`), or it is a format name (one of `html`, `xml`, `man`, `commonmark`, `markdown`, `text`, or `latex`), the output will be text. For example:

```
fuse('$\\pi$ = `{r} pi`', output = 'foo.pdf')  # file
mark('foo.md', output = NA)  # text
mark('foo.md', output = 'latex')  # text (LaTeX code)

```

If the input is a file and the `output` argument takes a file extension (one of `.html`, `.xml`, `.man`, `.markdown`, `.md`, `.txt`, or `.tex`),14
14 Note that the format name `commonmark` and the extension `.markdown` are for CommonMark output. It should be rare that you need to convert Markdown input to CommonMark output, but it is possible anyway. 
you will get a file output with the same base name as the input file. The `.pdf` extension can also be used if you want to convert Markdown to LaTeX, and then render LaTeX to PDF. For example: 
```
mark('foo/bar.md', output = '.html')  # file 'foo/bar.html'
mark('foo/bar.md', output = '.pdf')   # file 'foo/bar.pdf'

```

For `fuse()`, the `output` argument can take a special value of the form `markdown:format`, where `format` is a format name. This value means to generate the intermediate Markdown text for the target output format, e.g.,

```
fuse('foo.Rmd', output = 'markdown:latex')

```

Depending on the target output format, the intermediate Markdown may be different, e.g., for `html` output, the default graphical device is PNG, whereas for `latex` output, the default device is PDF.
[6](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:input-output "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#tab:input-output") provides a summary of the above rules.
6 The returned value for different combinations of `input` and `output` arguments of `mark()` / `fuse()`.  
| function  | `input`  | `output`  | returned value  |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  `mark()` / `fuse()`  | file  | `NULL`  | file  |  
|   | text  | `NULL`  | text  |  
|   | *  | file  | file  |  
|   | *  |  `NA` / `format`  | text  |  
|   | `file.xxx`  | `.ext`  | `file.ext`  |  
|   | `file.xxx`  | `.pdf`  | `file.pdf`  |  
| `fuse()`  | *  | `markdown`  | intermediate Markdown text  |  
|   | *  | `.md`  | intermediate Markdown file  |  
|   | *  | `markdown:format`  | intermediate Markdown text for `format`  |  
##  A.3 Package vignettes [](https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:vignettes "https://pkg.yihui.org/litedown/book/#sec:vignettes")
To build package vignettes with **litedown** , first add this to the package `DESCRIPTION` file:

```
VignetteBuilder: litedown

```

If your package does not require **litedown** as a hard dependency (specified in `Depends` or `Imports`), you will need to add it to `Suggests` in `DESCRIPTION`:

```
Suggests: litedown

```

Then use the vignette engine `litedown::vignette` in the YAML metadata of a `.Rmd` or `.md` vignette file:

```
vignette: >
  %\VignetteEngine{litedown::vignette}
  %\VignetteIndexEntry{Your vignette title}
  %\VignetteEncoding{UTF-8}

```

The output format of a vignette can be specified in the `output` field of the YAML metadata, e.g., `html` (for HTML vignettes) or `latex` (for PDF vignettes). If no output format is specified, the default is HTML.
The vignette file can be either `.Rmd` or `.md`. The former is processed by `litedown::fuse()`, and the latter is converted by `litedown::mark()`. Please avoid using the same base filename for two `.Rmd` and `.md` files, otherwise their output files will overwrite each other.
